# Changelog

Per-version execution narrative for mosko-fintech. Each entry documents what landed in a given WORKFLOW.md version: PR work, structured decision tracking (Q-N answers, vote outcomes), sub-section locks, ADR acceptance events.

**Not auto-loaded.** Consult on demand when answering "when did X land?" or "what happened in v1.NN?" questions.

**Distinct from `DECISIONS.md`** (architectural decisions with full rationale) per the [ADR-009](DECISIONS.md#adr-009) Decision 8 hybrid policy: CHANGELOG records WHAT/WHEN; DECISIONS records WHY/HOW IT REASONED.

**Format.** Newest at top. Each entry: `### vN.NN — YYYY-MM-DD` header followed by narrative paragraphs.

**Extracted from `WORKFLOW.md`** on 2026-05-23 per [ADR-009](DECISIONS.md#adr-009) Decision 6 implementation (task #10). 73 version entries total (v0.1 → v1.67).

---

### v1.185 — 2026-08-10

**Phase 6 — the [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) migration landed (PR #389, merge `c2a34ff`): `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication` + the one-helper gap policy + a 98-assertion battery, in one PR.** `063` creates the global append-only record of BLS periods published without a usable value; `064` creates `fn_cpi_u_index_for_period`, `SECURITY INVOKER`, returning a **six-column row and never a scalar** so that discarding carried-ness requires an explicit, diff-visible projection. **`pfin.cpi_u_index` itself is untouched**, as Option C ruled. All 15 CI checks green including the pgTAP battery.

**Sec returned AMBER with four blocking conditions and two measured-false claims; all cleared.** The review is recorded here rather than compressed to "Sec approved," because in every case the finding was **measurement correcting a plausible artifact**, and the artifacts read as considered before they were measured.

**⚠ The durable one — a fence described by observing it where it is reachable.** `063`'s header asserted the immutability triggers were *"the ONLY fence that holds against a privileged context."* Measured under the real roles: under `service_role`, UPDATE / DELETE / TRUNCATE / `on conflict do update` all fail at **`permission denied for table`** — **the ACL refuses first and the triggers never fire**; and the table owner *can* `alter table … disable trigger` and mutate. The claim originated in a true observation — the triggers **did** fire against a superuser — but they fired **because the owner's ACL does not refuse**, i.e. in the one context where a trigger is reachable at all. **The inference generalized a fence's behaviour from the only place it could be observed to the places it cannot.** The same false premise had propagated to **seven** sites including two catalog comments and the `revoke truncate` rationale; all seven were corrected coherently rather than patching the two quoted lines.

**⚠ The other durable one — a requirement that was correct and unexecutable.** `063` instructed a future writer to reuse the CPI-U mapper's month projection but not its value filter, since the value filter drops exactly the rows this table exists to record. True, and insufficient: **`fetch_cpi_df` discards value-null rows from its own return value** at `utils.py:513–515`, so the subject rows never leave the transport and there is nothing to apply the projection to. **A writer following the documented requirement gets an empty record, has discharged the stated obligation, and has no reason to look further** — an omission invites a question; a false sense of completion answers it in advance. Corrected to two obstacles in order, with the transport drop first and marked not-optional, plus the enforceable half: a **count reconciliation** the writer's run asserts — *transport periods = rows upserted + rows recorded* — failing loud on imbalance. ⚠ **Architect had found this obstacle in the prior pass and put it in a message rather than in the artifact**, leaving the artifact instructing something already known to be unexecutable; the block is the **sole durable home of that ruling**, since the ADR amendment carrying it was deliberately closed unmerged. *Documents are the memory; a chat message is not a document.*

**Other conditions: a one-sided bound, and a signature presented as settled.** The helper bounded the trailing edge and not the leading one, so **every period before `053`'s 2015 coverage start classified as `unrecorded_gap`** — the alarm class — for periods that were never due. Disposed by adding a `before_coverage` member rather than documenting it away, because the class set is consumer-visible and being locked at zero consumers; `unrecorded_gap` now means **strictly interior** and is the only alarm class. And the return shape is marked **PROVISIONAL**: Decision 4's full sentence reads *"the exact signature is the implementing PR's call **pending the product ruling**; the composite return and the non-silence it enforces are **not**"* — locked and mutable split deliberately, with the signature on the mutable side. ⚠ Necessary because the `comment on function` **has a database representation**: reshaping it after merge costs a comment-only migration rather than an edit.

**The `service_role` EXECUTE grant is recorded as a decision, not left silent.** EXECUTE is granted to `authenticated` only — deliberate on grounds rather than preference, since both tables are pure-global and [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) Step 0 assigns pure-global reads to `authenticated`, and a `service_role` grant would open a second read path for data that rule assigns to the first while buying nothing (`using (true)` leaves no RLS to bypass). **No caller is shut out** — the worker identity is a member of both roles per `055`, measured end-to-end. The fix for anyone who hits this is **`SET ROLE`, never widening the grant**, and both error shapes are named because the bare-NOINHERIT case fails **earlier**, at schema USAGE, and would otherwise be filed as an unrelated bug.

**The battery's contribution is that it varies the login role** — `anon` / `authenticated` / `service_role` / owner, each proven positively and negatively — which is precisely what the migration's own verification could not do, having run as `postgres` throughout. Non-vacuity is **encoded rather than reported**: every high-value negative breaks its fence inside a savepoint, asserts the flip, and rolls back, so the reproduction re-runs on every CI pass. ⚠ **One finding generalises past this PR:** an inversion leg **calibrates a primary assertion and never tracks the contract**. Measured — running the pre-extension battery against the corrected migration turned three legs red, and the return-shape *inversion* was **not** among them, because it replaces the function with its own mutant and asserts *that mutant's* signature. **A green inversion leg is not evidence the contract is unchanged.** Leg ordering was also fixed so a signature change yields one named red instead of a ~40-line `current transaction is aborted` cascade.

**⚠ `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication` ships GUARANTEED-EMPTY, filed as `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 S21 — ONE item with two halves that must ship together.** Emptiness is correct today because it is the only reachable state; the instant the transport drop is lifted **without** the count reconciliation, "empty" silently changes meaning from *correct* to *broken* and **no test, constraint, or layer would notice**. Also filed: **S22** — stalled-ingest detection is assigned to **no owner anywhere**, a gap surfaced by *withdrawing a false belief* rather than by new work (the verdict accepting the data-derived bound had cited an ADR clause as assigning it; the clause assigns nothing, and the overstatement was conceded on cross-check). And **S23** — two `053` defects: a false `source` column comment needing a comment-only migration, and a missing first-of-month CHECK whose absence lets one malformed row change another period's value **and** its classification at once.

**Ledger deltas: §10 catalogued instances +0 · cross-tenant FK-bypass family +0 · `SECURITY DEFINER` allowlist +0.** Non-membership in the FK-bypass family holds on two independent grounds — no FK-shaped column exists at all, and both tables are global with no tenant anchor — recorded together because the ADR states that a reader re-deriving only one would conclude the other was missed.

**Bookkeeping alongside the merge:** `V1-ship-block` added to the CPI-U overlay backend issue after the flag was **confirmed true** by a fresh read (a ship-blocking chart had been gated on a non-ship-blocking dependency); V1.1 issue state **re-queried against live Linear** — 6 Done / 8 Backlog / 0 Parked, reconciling the ledger's 6 / 7 / 1 once the F/CTO-side parked annotation on the backfill issue is accounted for. ⚠ **An anchor convention was swept:** labels of the form `2.1.2.c` / `2.1.2.d` are **Linear AC sub-letters, not PRD sections** — they appear zero times in the PRD — and written with a `§` they read as PRD anchors and pass every spot-check of their parts.

**⚠ Open and F/CTO-executed by construction: six advisory CI fences are ratified for promotion to branch-protection required checks and are NOT YET APPLIED** (`pgTAP RLS battery` · `Live-DB integration lane` · `Unit + typecheck + build` · `RT-27` · `TBC-node` · `tz-sweep`), deliberately excluding `Scheduled dep audit — liveness`, which asserts external cron state. The team-lead's permission classifier refuses branch-protection writes. **Until it lands, the pgTAP battery is a Sec merge gate enforced only by a human reading the result** — QA asked that its sign-off be described as **advisory rather than enforced**, on the grounds that the failure mode is not a red nobody blocks on but **a red nobody reads**.

### v1.184 — 2026-08-10

**Phase 6 — `MILESTONES.md` `## Pending (immediate)` refreshed to current, and the repo swept to `main` alone (PR #388).** Doc-only; **no code, no migration, no schema surface touched.** The CURRENT block had been anchored at `main` = `5d26c08` / v1.170 since 2026-08-09 and had accumulated thirteen versions of drift while the head note read v1.183 — so a fresh session's SessionStart auto-load presented a stale anchor as current state.

**⚠ The drift was not merely stale, it was FALSE, and it read as measurement.** The block carried a table headed *"The §2.1.2 chain, measured 2026-08-09"* whose six cells a reader would reasonably trust. **Three had gone false in the interim:** `pfin.cpi_u_index` **0 rows** (actually 137), `pfin.nav_daily` **0 rows** (actually one real checkpoint, per S10), and `pfin_etl` **inert, `rolcanlogin=f`** (actually granted a real LOGIN, used for the backfill, and revoked afterwards). The three surviving cells — `fn_nav_series` built at `062`, the chart not built, cron not wired — are re-stated as still true rather than silently retained, and the correction is labelled **SUPERSEDED** in place rather than the table being deleted, because *the shape of the error is the transferable part: a paragraph that says "measured" ages into a false assertion with no signal that it has.*

**⚠ The `2025-10` note FLIPPED, and the direction matters.** It previously read *"most likely a BLS publication gap … but that is **recall, not measurement**"* and instructed a reader to go confirm the source gap. That confirmation has since happened ([ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049), measured at `4a5a9ad` by direct query rather than inferred from the row count): BLS published the period with `value = '-'`, and `053`'s `NOT NULL` **forbids storing it**, so the drop is **forced and correct**. The note is rewritten from an open question into a settled fact **carrying an explicit do-NOT-"fix" instruction** — the failure mode it now guards against is a future session reading `137 ≠ 138` as an importer defect and repairing something that is already right. **The binding half survives unchanged: do not assume contiguous months.**

**Seven discharged "Owed" bullets removed; three standing constraints preserved.** Every bullet in the `Owed, by owner:` list had been discharged 2026-08-09 and was being carried as strikethrough, which made a list of *nothing owed* look like a list of work. They are removed with a pointer to CHANGELOG v1.170 and [ADR-045](DECISIONS.md#adr-045) / [ADR-046](DECISIONS.md#adr-046) / [ADR-008](DECISIONS.md#adr-008)'s 2026-08-09 amendment, and the removal is **stated in the block** rather than done silently. ⚠ **Three of those bullets carried standing constraints that are not bookkeeping** — the `service_role` double-fence DO-NOT-TOUCH, ADR-046's accept-despite-feasible residual with its re-open triggers, and the measurement-independent `ALTER ROLE … PASSWORD` prohibition. Those are promoted into a new **`Standing constraints`** sub-list under a heading that says what they have in common: *each presents as something safe to tidy.* The new `Owed` list holds the four items actually outstanding.

**The forward path is stated as an ordered list for the first time:** (1) the [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) migration — `063`+, Architect authors, **Sec joint-review is a MERGE GATE** per [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1, QA pairs the pgTAP battery; (2) the **PM product half** — ⚠ routed to **[PRD §2.4.4](docs/PRD/index.html)'s non-silent-staleness principle** per [ADR-013](DECISIONS.md#adr-013) and explicitly **NOT to "INV-1"**, an unrelated §2.6 injection invariant the two were conflated with once already; (3) the §2.1.2.c CPI-U overlay backend; (4) the §2.1.2.d NAV chart. **ETL gates none of it** — the 2026-08-09 direction to clear the defect stack first is recorded discharged.

**Repo branch hygiene: 22 branches deleted, `main` now stands alone.** One unmerged remote (the closed #384 branch) and one unmerged local (the closed #386 branch) were verified as carrying nothing canon needs — #384's text was **measured false** and #386's ruling **shipped as code at #387** instead of as an ADR — then deleted alongside 21 merged-PR leftovers. ⚠ **Recovery is deliberate, not incidental:** GitHub retains `refs/pull/<N>/head` permanently, and `refs/pull/386/head` was verified byte-identical to the deleted tip before anything was removed, so the measured refutation of the `S01`/`S02`-survive premise stays reachable exactly where a future reader meeting the false version will land. **`delete_branch_on_merge` flipped `true`** on the repository so merged PRs self-clean — ⚠ **with the limit recorded in the block: a PR closed WITHOUT merging still leaves its branch, and a closed branch usually means a decision was made about its content, so the close comment is read before the branch is deleted.**

**`Recent activity` brought current under its own convention.** The section is capped at five entries and was listing v1.166–v1.170, omitting the entire ETL arc; it now carries the five most recent (v1.184 · the ADR-049 + parse-boundary landings · v1.183 · v1.182 · v1.170).

### v1.183 — 2026-08-10

**Phase 6 — the ETL defect stack CLOSED: `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 has no open ETL defect (PRs #380, #381).** **S15 discharged** (#381) — automap named a generated relationship after the **referred table**, so `pfin.lot_match`'s two FKs to `pfin.account_trans` produced the same name twice and the second silently overwrote the first, with **no error and no warning**, unlike S13. ⚠ **Both sides collapsed INDEPENDENTLY**, which is the non-obvious part: the scalar resolved to the **SELL** leg while its *nominal reverse* collection resolved to the **BUY** leg, so **they were not reverses of each other** and fixing only the scalar would have left a mapping that still lied, more quietly. Each relationship now takes a **meaning-carrying name from its own FK columns**; **mapper-attribute only — no DDL, no migration, Decision 3 untouched**. The **S13 guard correctly declined to act** (it fires on a relationship-vs-*column* collision, and `lot_match` has no `account_trans` column — **nothing was claimed, so nothing looked wrong**), which is why this needed a separate condition rather than a widening. **Scope re-measured at the fix with the applied head verified unmoved**, not re-cited; **blast radius was zero at the time of fixing**, which is why it was cheap and why it was never urgent. **The tests construct the generality they claim** — three-FK, no-colliding-column, reverse-side and single-FK non-intervention cases — because the live schema exercises the guard exactly once. **S20 filed and discharged** (#380) — a BLS period published with **no value** (measured live: `2025-M10`, a real non-publication returned as `"-"`) was dropped **silently**. ⚠ **The drop was never the defect:** `053`'s `NOT NULL` + finiteness CHECK make storing a valueless period impossible, so **the schema forces it**. What was wrong is that **nothing at any layer distinguished *"BLS published no value"* from *"we lost a row"***, and `cpi_u_index` asserts nothing about contiguity — so the gap was **undetectable by construction** and propagated into inflation-adjusted figures. ⚠ **The diagnosis named the wrong mechanism** — the expected culprit (`clean_empty_str_df`) is **not on that path at all**; the drop is in `fetch_cpi_df`, one layer earlier — so *following the pointer rather than the mechanism would have missed it.* Fixed with a **per-row warning** stating the gap is real and **not a fetch failure** (a log that merely announces a drop trades silence for a **false alarm**, inviting someone to "fix" a correct drop) plus an **unconditional count reconciliation**, which is the load-bearing half: a per-row warning is still silent about a *systematic* drop, and emitting the reconciliation only on a drop would make its absence ambiguous — *"nothing dropped"* and *"the logging broke"* would look identical. **The consumer contract was routed, not resolved in code: F/CTO ruled Option C** — the value table stays strictly non-null, with a separate observation record for published-but-unusable periods. **⚠ S19 is deliberately NOT struck:** its **code half shipped** (the `BLS_API_KEY_TEST` loader preference) but its **practice half has not** — the local `.env` still holds the key under the **production** name, so *"is there a production-named credential on this laptop?"* still answers **YES**. **Marking it closed because the code shipped would be exactly the shape of claiming coverage a change does not provide**, on an entry whose own subject is a convention that holds only if practice follows it. **⚠ Recorded as a shape: the ledger sweep at v1.182 was stale on arrival** — it preceded #381, so S15 read open the moment it merged. *A ledger swept before the last fix lands is stale on arrival, and the sweep looks complete at the time.*

### v1.182 — 2026-08-10

**Phase 6 — the ETL defect stack CLEARED end to end, and `pfin.cpi_u_index` populated by the real worker under a real login (PRs #360–#378).** A single catch-up entry covering the 18 PRs merged since v1.181, because the work is one arc and a trail of per-PR entries would obscure it. **The arc: `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 went from S11–S18 open to S15 alone open, and the CPI-U table went 0 → 137 rows.** **S17 fixed** — `SBaseConn` never assumed a database role, so every statement, **reads included**, failed `42501` under the production login; `055` grants `pfin_etl` no direct privileges and creates it NOINHERIT, and `set local role service_role` existed in `nav_daily.py` alone. Fixed with a `_role()` context manager assumed **explicitly at each call site** (**required role argument with no default**, an **allowlist that doubles as the injection fence** since `SET ROLE` cannot be parameterised, and `impersonate()`'s N1 teardown so a failed `reset role` cannot replace the original exception); **reads `authenticated`, writes `service_role`** per the [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) amendment (#363). **⚠ The fix as first merged (#372) was INCOMPLETE and only the MANUAL acceptance run found it:** `metadata.reflect()` sits in `__init__`, **ahead of every data path**, so construction died at `permission denied for schema pfin` — **one gate earlier than the table privileges S17 was filed against** — while **every automated check passed on the unfixed code** (#377). **A fix covering every statement except the first one covers nothing.** **Proven in both directions by an operator under a real `pfin_etl` LOGIN:** RED `42501`, then `count = 0` after `set role service_role` — *same session, same credential*; GREEN **0 → 137 rows**; and a second run held the count flat with `ingested_at` **re-stamped**, proving the `ON CONFLICT` path executed rather than no-opping. **⚠ `update_table_df` was REPAIRED, not merely hardened:** staging was seeded `AS SELECT * FROM target`, so every updated key matched **two** staging rows and the `UPDATE … FROM` join — documented as unpredictable — **took the stale one**; measured, `UPDATE 3` where 2 were intended, **both updates silently discarded**, every untouched row self-updated. **The FMP price-refresh path did not reliably update anything.** Latent rather than an incident: `eod_price` held **zero rows** and is the only live V1 caller. **S12 fixed** (#369) — credential loading split so the loader raises for nothing and `require_api_key()` raises at the **point of use**; the NAV cron no longer needs two keys it never uses, and the AC is now **enforced in CI hermetically** (SQLAlchemy engines are lazy, so construction needs no database). **S16's actionable half fixed** (#364, #366) — and it took **three iterations**, because extracting the policy and testing it left the fence **removable with a green suite**: nothing asserted the fixtures *called* it. **⚠ The third layer generalises past this repo — `pytest.skip()` raises a `BaseException` that `pytest.raises` does not catch, so a test guarding against improper skipping is itself silently SKIPPABLE and the suite stays green.** pgTAP is structurally immune (`select plan(N)` turns an unrun assertion into a **failure**); pytest has no plan count. A **second, ungoverned skip policy** was found and reconciled — written *in reaction to* the over-broad `except`, it reproduced a milder form of it. **S14 ruled KEYED** ([ADR-048](DECISIONS.md#adr-048), #365) — the architecture corrected to the code, not the reverse. **S18 discharged** — `run_cpi_backfill.py` committed, and the acceptance run was executed **through it**, which is what makes a discharge reproducible by someone other than its author. **S19 filed** — the loader accepted only the production BLS name, so a dev machine necessarily held a production-named credential; now `BLS_API_KEY_TEST` is preferred (#367 + #369). **⚠ Explicitly NOT a fence** — nothing can inspect a local `.env`; what it buys is auditability **by shape rather than trust**. **A `pgtest` tier was added** (#375) — needs real Postgres, **no credentials, no schema** — after the false dichotomy *hermetic-SQLite vs manual-integration* was corrected: the property those tests need is **real Postgres, not manual**. **Two path-blind measurements were retracted:** the local stack's `:54322` is a **Docker NAT hop**, so a host client presents as `192.168.65.1` and can never traverse the `127.0.0.1/32 trust` line — `055`'s claims were right on their own path, and the apparent refutations came from an instrument that was never on it (#371). **The durable lesson: a refutation needs the same path-validity check as a proof** — every near-miss that reached the point of editing an artifact was a *disproof*, because a negative result feels like it needs less justification. **Seven concealment layers in one stack, each invisible to the layer above it, and the seventh invisible to the fix for the sixth. The only thing that reliably found them was a human running the assembled path** — which is the argument S10 was filed to make, now demonstrated twice.

### v1.181 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 ledger corrected: S11 struck as already-discharged, and two new entries filed — S17 (⚠ PRODUCTION-FATAL) + S18.** Doc-only; **no code, no migration** — one `workers/CLAUDE.md` prose correction, no executable `workers/` file touched. **S11 was fixed on `main` and the ledger did not say so.** `a85c9f5` (PR #355) is an ancestor of `main`; the transport parameter reads from `<prefix>DB_SSLMODE`, resolves against an allowlist of libpq's six modes, and **defaults to `require`** — unset/absent/blank all resolve to `require`, only an explicit value can weaken it, and an unrecognised one **raises rather than falling back**. **S10's "Deviation" bullet had gone on asserting the hard-code in the present tense for two merges** and is now past-tense **without deleting the history** — while its other half, **that TLS transport was never exercised end to end, survives the edit intact and is explicitly marked as *not* closed by S11**: making a parameter configurable is a verifiability fix, not a TLS-terminating stack. ⚠ **One residual named rather than rounded up:** S11's AC also called for a `workers/etl` README note; the env plumbing landed in `.env.example:178–185` (arguably the better home) but **the README carries no `sslmode` mention at all** — recorded as an open doc-only residual instead of counted as met. **S17 filed, ⚠ PRODUCTION-FATAL: `SBaseConn` never assumes a database role.** `053` grants writes to `service_role`, and `055` **deliberately** grants `pfin_etl` no direct table privileges while creating it **NOINHERIT**, so the session holds nothing until an explicit `SET ROLE` — and `set local role service_role` exists in **`nav_daily.py` alone** (grep-verified); `TenantBoundConnection.system()` sets no role. **Every `SBaseConn` statement therefore fails `42501` under the production login — reads included**, not merely writes, since NOINHERIT `pfin_etl` holds neither role's SELECT ambiently. **Why it stayed invisible is the part worth keeping:** every local run authenticates as **`postgres`**, a superuser needing no role assumption, so **the defect is invariant under every run anyone has performed**; and **S10 exercised `nav_daily` — the one worker that does assume the role.** That is the third instance in this section of *verifying through whichever path is easiest to reach, which is systematically the least representative one.* ⚠ **The trap is recorded explicitly: a local backfill as `postgres` will succeed and will populate `pfin.cpi_u_index`, and rows in that table are not evidence the importer works** — the next reader must ask which role wrote them. **The AC names the wrong-but-tempting fix and forbids it** (an engine-level listener confers ambient privilege for the engine's lifetime and defeats `055`'s NOINHERIT choice), requires **RED first** under a real `pfin_etl` LOGIN before the fix, and carries one measured unknown — whether `service_role` holds **TEMP**, which `_staging_update` needs and which would be an **Architect** migration rather than a Backend fix. **Sec joint-review is a MERGE GATE** ([ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1, privileged-context-write); the fix authors no migration, changes no RLS, moves no §10 catalogued instance and expands no Decision 3 instance. **S18 filed:** `backfill_cpi_u_index` has **zero callers** outside one integration test — `run_nav_daily.py` exists, `run_cpi_backfill.py` does not — so the only ways to run it are the fixture whose over-broad `except` is S16's actionable half, or **an uncommitted scratchpad**, which is precisely the shape S10 named and which S11 is this section's worked example of the cost of. **Also recorded: `update_table_all()` calls `update_table_cpi()` — targeting `pfin.cpi`, created by no V1 migration — *before* the CPI-U step, so the container entry point cannot reach CPI-U even once S17 is fixed.** **S16 amended:** narrowing its fixture's `except` is **necessary and not sufficient** — the other half of the concealment is that the lane only ever runs as a superuser, and **a suite that passes under every value of a variable is blind to that variable, not robust to it**; the variable is the login role, not the exception class, so narrowing alone would have left S17 exactly as invisible. **Also corrected: `workers/CLAUDE.md`'s ETL-CI line**, which said coverage was *"a tracked follow-up, not yet wired"* while `etl-ci.yml` exists. **Measured clause by clause, because the conclusion survives although two of its three premises do not:** ruff ✅ (on `src/` only) and pytest ✅ (**`-m unit` only**) are wired; the **uv-aware dep audit is still absent** — `scheduled-dep-audit.yml` enumerates `git ls-files '*package.json'`, so **no Python manifest is audited at all**; and ⚠ **`test_replay_parity.py`, the line's own named "first-green target", still does not run** — it is marked `@pytest.mark.replay` and `-m unit` **deselects** it. **The load-bearing half is preserved and sharpened rather than erased:** the `integration` and `deployment` lanes are not in CI and cannot be, so **end-to-end evidence for this worker is manual and never a CI gate** — a green check means lint on `src/` plus the unit lane, and **S13, S16 and S17 were all green in CI throughout**. **Two planning outcomes recorded in the ledger so they are not re-litigated at implementation time:** **(i) S15's scope MEASURED** by read-only catalog query against a stack verified at head 62/62 — across both schemas automap reflects, **exactly ONE (child → parent) pair carries more than one FK** (`lot_match → account_trans`), which bounds the fix **and** means its guard is unexercised by any other pair, so the test must construct the generality it claims; re-measure rather than re-cite as migrations land. **(ii) S12's shape RULED** (team-lead): split `load_db_params()` / `load_api_keys()`, loader raises for nothing, key accessor raises at point of use — chosen over a `require=(...)` argument because forgetting that argument would **silently loosen** the check rather than trip it; **and S12's scope widened**, since `PFinBackend` carries the same defect plus an unconditional `PFinFMP` construction, so a CPI-only container must hold an FMP credential it never uses. **`cpi_u_index` remains 0 rows; the backfill remains unrun; product work is held behind the ETL stack per F/CTO direction.**

### v1.180 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 **S13** FIXED (PR #357, Backend-authored): `PFinBackend` constructs again; S15 + S16 filed.** The production-fatal automap collision is closed. `pfin.user_taxonomy` carried **both a column `tax_character` and an FK to a table `tax_character`**, so automap's generated relationship collided with the column and raised — blocking `main.py:48` and with it the entire `PFinBackend` surface. **Verified on `main` after merge:** `PFinBackend()` constructs, `.tax_character` is still the TEXT **column**, and the relationship moved to `.tax_character_ref`. **Fixed at the mapper, not the schema** — no migration, no DDL, 5 files all inside `workers/etl/`; `tax_character` is ratified domain vocabulary on a locked surface and the defect was in automap's naming. **The guard is general, not a special case: zero occurrences of `tax_character` in executable code** (verified by tokenising the source and stripping comments/docstrings) — it delegates to automap's own default, so upstream naming stays tracked, and renames **only** on a real collision. ⚠ **The design point worth keeping:** the fallback name is keyed on the **FK's own columns**, not a constant `_ref` suffix — a constant would collapse multiple FKs from one table to one target onto a single name, **trading S13's loud failure for a silent wrong one**, which is precisely the defect S15 records. Tests in two layers, **honestly labelled**: 10 hermetic `@unit` tests that protect `main`, and 2 `@integration` that need a database, are deselected by CI, and **therefore do not protect `main` on their own** — stated in the PR body and the file header rather than implied. Both shown **RED first** (hooks unwired → the exact ArgumentError; guard neutered → 7 of 10 red, with exactly the non-intervention assertions staying green), and one test pins that **upstream's default still produces the colliding name**, so the suite cannot pass vacuously. `pytest -m unit --strict-markers` → **144 passed**. **⚠ A fourth layer of concealment was measured and filed (S16):** `conftest.py`'s `backend` fixture wraps construction in `except Exception: pytest.skip(...)`, converting this **production-fatal** failure into a **green skip** — in the pre-fix run the existing fixture-based tests *skipped on the very error blocking the production entry point*. That is why "the ETL tests are green" carried no information about whether the ETL could start. **S15 filed:** `pfin.lot_match`'s **two** FKs to `pfin.account_trans` (`buy_trans_id` / `sell_trans_id`, both verified independently) collapse onto one relationship **with no error and no warning** — the scalar joins on `sell_trans_id`, the nominal reverse collection on `buy_trans_id`, so **they are not reverses of each other**. Arguably worse than S13, which at least failed loudly. **Blast radius today is zero** (grep-verified: the only `lot_match` mention in ETL source is a comment in S13's own guard), which is the argument for fixing it now rather than after a consumer depends on the wrong meaning. **S16 filed as a note, not a new defect**, recording that the integration lane's now-visible failures are **pre-existing incumbent-vs-V1 drift** already tracked with PM + Architect — so the next person to run it does not mistake a scope gap for an S13 regression. **Role note:** S13 was implemented by the **Backend Engineer**, not by team-lead, after F/CTO corrected a role-collapse — S11 (PR #355) had been written by team-lead while explicitly labelling itself "operating as Backend", which is the failure mode with a label on it.

### v1.179 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — `BACKLOG.md` §7.6 S11 FIXED (PR #355); the CPI-U backfill then hit a PRODUCTION-FATAL defect, filed as S13, plus S14.** **S11 landed:** `build_database_url` appended `?sslmode=require` unconditionally with no override, and the local Supabase CLI stack offers no TLS — so **the ETL could not be pointed at the local stack at all**, with the barrier at connect time before any logic. Now env-driven via `PFIN_DB_SSLMODE` with **`require` as the default**: unset / absent / blank all resolve to `require`, so production is unchanged and a forgetful environment still demands TLS; only an explicit libpq mode can weaken it, and an unrecognised value **raises rather than falling back** (falling back would change the transport posture without telling anyone — `"REQUIRE"` is rejected too, libpq being case-sensitive). **First test coverage for that function — 18 cases, proven to fail** (default flipped to `disable` → 4 red; bad values falling back → 6 red). ⚠ **The acceptance control was vacuous on the first attempt and is recorded as such:** it asserted on `NavDailyWorker` *construction*, but **SQLAlchemy engines are lazy**, so `require` was never exercised; redone against the connection, the default **fails** (*"server does not support SSL, but SSL was required"*) and only the explicit override connects. **Then the CPI-U backfill was attempted and could not start** — `PFinBackend()` raises during automap: `pfin.user_taxonomy` carries **both a column `tax_character` and an FK to a table `tax_character`**, so the generated relationship collides with the column. **Filed as S13, ⚠ PRODUCTION-FATAL:** `main.py:48` constructs `PFinBackend()` then calls `update_table_all()`, so **the production ETL entry point cannot start**, and every `PFinBackend` method — CPI-U import, FMP price ingest — is unreachable. **Not local-only:** automap reflects whatever schema it connects to, and production carries the same migrations. **Latent ~50 migrations** (introduced at `009` + `011`). **Why it stayed invisible is the instructive part:** `NavDailyWorker` has **zero** automap references — raw SQL via `TenantBoundConnection` — so **S10 passed while this was broken**; the one worker anyone had run was precisely the one that bypasses the defect, and S11's connection-level barrier sat in front of it so nobody could reach it to find out. **S14 filed** to reconcile the already-flagged ARCH-vs-code BLS discrepancy — ARCH §5 frames BLS as keyless while `utils.py` hard-raises without `BLS_API_KEY`; the key is a **rate-limit lift, not an access requirement** (BLS publishes a keyless v1), so the code turns an optional optimisation into a hard credential dependency — which forced a **production-classified credential to be borrowed for a local run** (F/CTO-authorised, but the borrow is what secrets non-overlap exists to avoid). **CPI-U backfill remains unrun; `cpi_u_index` still 0 rows. SELF-218 is blocked on S13.**

### v1.176 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — runbook §6.1's stale flag-#10 block retracted in place (Sec-confirmed).** Closes the last carried security item, and **the item itself was stale**: flag #10 was **resolved 2026-08-02 at Sec joint-review B10** and had been carried on the queue as open for three sessions. The remediation is committed — the atomic `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<plaintext>'`, which `055` *previously mandated*, is now **PROHIBITED**; `log_statement = ddl` was measured and Postgres does **not** redact, so that statement would have written the ETL credential to the server log in cleartext. Replaced by `\password` (verifier computed **client-side**, so plaintext reaches neither the wire, the server log, nor `~/.psql_history`) then `ALTER ROLE … LOGIN` (carries no secret). **The live defect this uncovered: the remediation left behind the block it falsified.** §6.1 still carried the original *"⚠ Open flag (#10) … Sec-review before this section locks"* text, contradicted four ways by content in its own section — the `'<literal>'` statement it cites *"above"* is gone (and its form is now prohibited 40 lines up), the stack-setting it says is unverified was measured, the mitigation it proposes weighing **is** `\password` already adopted, and the ledger row 100 lines below marks it **✅ resolved**. On a **🔒 SECURITY-SENSITIVE** section an operator reading top-to-bottom was told the question was open and to weigh a mitigation already in force. Retracted in place with the false text quoted, per the standing rule that *a change must not leave behind documentation its own change falsifies*. **⚠ Sec corrected the retraction before it landed, and the correction is the durable part:** a first draft said *"every clause is now false"* and *"`log_statement = ddl` **was** measured"* — both overstate. One clause (*"what the self-hosted Supabase stack sets"*) is **not false but moot**, because the V1 production stack does not exist and **has not been measured**; and an unqualified *"was measured"* invites the inference that measurement is what settles this, which §6.1 line 287 explicitly forbids — *"the prohibition does not depend on any per-stack measurement — do not measure a target, find logging off, and conclude it lapses."* **A retraction phrased that way would have reintroduced the failure-open reasoning the section was rewritten to block.** Landed as *"either false or moot"* with the measurement-independence stated inline. **Residual unchanged and accepted:** the SCRAM verifier **is** still logged; a verifier is not a usable credential (`StoredKey = H(ClientKey)` does not invert), and the B10 condition that the secret be high-entropy and machine-generated is what keeps the residual offline attack economically irrelevant. Ledger row at §6.1 needed no change. Doc-only; no code, no schema.

### v1.175 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — `BACKLOG.md` §7.10 filed: the cron-liveness branch harness (Sec's condition on PR #340).** Discharges the last of the A1-stream owed items. **Filed as a §7 entry rather than a PR-body follow-up number, per the standing rule that those are not trackers.** The `scheduled-audit-liveness` job's FATAL branches were *"exercised, three via a `gh` shim"* at authoring — **exercised, not committed**; nothing re-runs them. **⚠ The entry's load-bearing requirement is that the harness assert MESSAGES, not exit codes**, and the job already argues this in its own body: *"This job has many branches that all exit 1 … Asserting `"exit 1"` would therefore verify nothing here — only the parsed value discriminates."* A harness asserting *"the job exited 1"* against a job whose branches all exit 1 is the **fence-that-cannot-fail** shape arriving through the test instead of the fence — GREEN in a coverage review while distinguishing nothing. **Scoped NARROWER than the ledger had been carrying it:** the **parser self-test is already committed and runs on every invocation** (a case table asserting parsed values over malformed and well-formed status lines), so only the FATAL branches lack coverage. ⚠ **A count correction rides with it** — the queue had described this as *"the 13 liveness branches"*, a figure that could not be sourced and appears to conflate three families; measured 2026-08-09 the job carries **six** FATAL branches, **six** parser self-test cases and **one** success path, and PR #340's own body says *"All six branches exercised."* Per the derived-surface rule the filed entry therefore **carries no tally at all** and instructs the builder to enumerate by measurement (`grep -c 'echo "FATAL'` over the job block) rather than from the entry. AC requires the harness be **proven to fail** per branch — reproduce at authoring, do not port a green result, the RT-31 leg (i) standard — that the shim-only branches ship their shim committed, and that an empty branch list fail loudly rather than pass. **Scope discipline: deliberately does NOT extend to A1's own inversion probe**, which is committed, runs every invocation, and was validated in production when A1's first scheduled run caught a real advisory. **Not blocking** — this hardens the observer against silent degradation over months, not anything live today.

### v1.173 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — [ADR-046](DECISIONS.md#adr-046) ACCEPTED: privileged write-path tenant fencing dispositioned.** Closes both items the axis (iv) sweep opened. **(1) The checkpoint tables' write-tenant residual is EXPLICITLY ACCEPTED for V1**, no fence built. **(2) `mfa_recovery_*` needs none** — posture correct as designed per [ADR-030](DECISIONS.md#adr-030), which records the alternative as rejected. **The transferable part is why the two differ.** The SELF-214 B7 (c′) fence works because the writer **impersonates**, so the tenant GUC is captured from `auth.uid()` *as the database resolved it* — **that precondition is a property of the WRITE PATH, not of the table's column shape.** Measured: `TenantBoundClient` (checkpoints) does impersonate → (c′) applicable; `supabaseAdmin()` (MFA) is a plain client with no impersonation → `auth.uid()` and `request.jwt.claims` both **NULL** under bare `service_role` → **(c′) impossible to fail there, and Sec would veto it if proposed.** ⚠ **Team-lead proposed extending (c′) to `mfa_recovery_*` on a column-shape match (`users_id` a direct writer-supplied anchor, same as `nav_daily`) and was WRONG** — the shape matched and the mechanism did not; one measurement killed it. **What is unfenced at the checkpoints, precisely:** `holdings_checkpoint_security_asset` (Decision-3 instance **#11**) asserts the asset and account agree *with each other*; nothing asserts the account belongs to **the tenant the worker was serving**, so a worker impersonating tenant A writing against tenant B's account and B's asset passes every existing fence. **⚠ Accept-despite-feasible, not accept-because-impossible** — remediation is cheap and the precedent is in the same table, whose trigger body states *"LOAD-BEARING UNDER service_role … this trigger is the SOLE fence."* Accepted on severity **flag, not veto**: single-user V1, our worker, `TenantBoundClient` binds in application code, and reaching the failure needs an application bug. **⚠ One premise of that severity rating was measurably FALSE and the rating is re-grounded rather than inherited** — Sec rated low partly on *"no second anchor to mismatch, so not a Decision-3 instance"*, but `holdings_checkpoint` **has** a second anchor (`security_id` → `pfin.asset`, which carries `users_id`) and **is** catalogued instance #11; the conclusion survives on the better reason that the second anchor is *already fenced*. **⚠ Honest limit recorded: the gap is INFERRED from fence absence, not demonstrated** — nobody constructed a cross-tenant checkpoint write, and whether `TenantBoundClient`'s callers can even reach a mismatched `account_id` was not established. Re-open triggers: a second user · any new writer of these tables · a demonstrated cross-tenant write. **No artifact change owed** — §4.1 already states coverage is non-uniform and that a surface is safe only where a mechanism is demonstrated at its own row; the checkpoint rows demonstrate none, so **the silence is the signal and must not be tidied.** Two MFA items noted and not actioned: the durable control there would be **code-layer**, not a DB trigger; and `026`'s table-wide UPDATE grant could be column-scoped to `used_at` to match its own neighbouring precedent (**marginal gain deliberately not inflated** — `service_role` already holds unrestricted INSERT). **Governance FLAT: §10 = 3 · Decision-3 = 16 labeled / 13 DDL-realized (#11 referenced, not moved) · DEFINER allowlist = 4 committed / 3 authored.** No migration, no DDL. The rewrite retitles the axis to the **JWT-authenticated tier**, keeps the measured-true `authenticated` claim and the manual-entry-integrity remainder, and adds that V1 has a **second write tier this axis does not cover** — privileged non-JWT writers under `service_role`, whose tenant correctness rests on application-layer binding, Decision-3 matched-tenant triggers (**not** bypassed by `BYPASSRLS`), and per-surface DB fences where authored — with **coverage explicitly stated as non-uniform**. Sec recommended the amendment **and stated the counter-argument against itself**; F/CTO ruled for it. **⚠ Discloses rather than closes a real asymmetry:** `nav_daily` got its fence at SELF-214 *because Sec argued application-layer binding was insufficient and F/CTO ratified that*, and the (c′) reasoning was never extended to `account_balance_checkpoint` / `holdings_checkpoint`, written by the same worker tier under the same role. **Flag, not veto** — single-user V1, our worker, and the FK topology fully determines those rows' tenant via `account_id` (no second anchor, so not a Decision-3 instance). Extend-or-accept is a separate Architect + F/CTO decision, deliberately unsettled, and the artifact must not phrase around it. **Open and unresolved, Sec-flagged uncertain:** `mfa_recovery_code` / `mfa_recovery_attempt` carry no policies and no fence, write posture unestablished. **Governance FLAT: §10 = 3 · Decision-3 = 16 labeled / 13 DDL-realized · DEFINER allowlist = 4 committed / 3 authored**; no count superseded, no §10 attribution moved, Sec §10 3-axis cross-check CLEAN. **No veto.**

The exposure generalizes because both bypass mechanisms are properties of the mechanism rather than of `nav_value`: `pg_read_all_data` membership grants SELECT irrespective of this project's GRANTs, and ownership bypasses because the `pfin` tables are not `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` (measured: 27 tables, RLS on 27, FORCE on **0**). **The sharp finding: localizing the bound made the unbounded rows read MORE strongly than before it landed** — sixteen uniformly-silent claims became one explicitly tier-scoped and fifteen not, so the silence of the fifteen reads as deliberate contrast. A safety proof functioning as someone else's hazard notice. Fixed with **one Path-B statement at §4.1**, propagating by the artifact's own declared inheritance (*"all assume the §4.1 isolation primitive as foundation"*) rather than fanned into fifteen rows that would be one claim copied fifteen ways. **[ADR-045](DECISIONS.md#adr-045) records the vehicle ruling** — plain doc edit, **no ADR-008 amendment** — on two legs: stating a scope that always held mutates nothing (SD-24's F/CTO-ratified *"and it never was"*), and ADR-008's amendment trigger is an **exhaustive five-item enumeration** a tier qualifier does not join **provided it commits to nothing**. ⚠ **The wording chooses the vehicle**: phrased as a commitment it becomes the fifth trigger, so SD-24 control (i)'s row-level commitment must stay at row level. Sec's seven constraints bind the bound, of which two are load-bearing — **no remediation framing** (RLS was never a control here; *"and here is how we close it"* would be worse than none), and **RT-31 must not be presented as closing the gap** (it detects *growth* of the bypass-capable set; it does not remove the residual). **Provenance recorded because it changed a verdict:** the ledger entry that carried this item to Sec quoted §4.1 as calling RLS *"V1's sole tenant-isolation primitive"* — a string that has never existed in the artifact (retracted at PR #345) — and **Sec recorded that had it inherited that framing it would have ruled the other way, and wrongly.** A fabricated quotation does not merely mis-cite; it re-sizes the finding upward, because an invented sentence is written to make its point. **Left open, deliberately unfolded:** §4.1 axis (iv) (*"No write path in V1 bypasses the tenant-scoping the read path enforces"*) may be falsified by the W-1 worker, which writes as `service_role` with scoping enforced by a GUC fence rather than the RLS policy layer — Sec **uncertain and self-labelled unmeasured**; a different tier, hence a different remedy. **Governance FLAT: §10 = 3 · Decision-3 = 16 labeled / 13 DDL-realized · DEFINER allowlist = 4 committed / 3 authored.** Sec §10 3-axis cross-check CLEAN; **no veto, nothing blocked** — a documented-residual disclosure improvement.

### v1.170 — 2026-08-09

**Phase 6 — RT-31 leg (i) AUTHORED: the bypass-capable role-set fence (PR #343, merge `5d26c08`).** The standing regression detector for the **SD-24 tier boundary**, specified by Sec at PR #341 and carried forward unauthored since SELF-219. 11 assertions `(i0)`–`(i10)` in `054_nav_daily_rls.sql`; `plan(63)` → `plan(74)`; battery **`Files=59, Tests=1201, PASS`**. **No migration, no application code.** SD-24's *"never cross-tenant"* claim is scoped to the **application tier**; the DB-admin tier reads across tenants and that residual is documented and non-remediable. What this project owns is that the bypass-capable set **has not grown** — five platform roles hold it, and a sixth arriving has no application-tier symptom at all. **Set-complement shaped per Sec's mandate** — `is_empty()` over a query that *returns* the offending rows, never a per-role deny-list, the direct remediation of the recorded failure *"I asked a question that could not return the roles I hadn't thought of, and read its silence as absence."* **One deviation, flagged rather than buried and ruled by Sec:** the mandated query has ONE definition with three call sites rather than three literal copies, because `(i2)`/`(i3)` exist to prove `(i1)`'s green means something and a drifted copy would leave the probes validating a query the fence no longer runs — the PR #330 TZ-sweep defect. Sec ruled the literal form was never mandated (the `Shape:` line is a template carrying `<allowlist>` placeholders) and that **three literal copies would have been a finding from it**. **Non-vacuity is re-proven every run rather than argued:** `(i2)`/`(i3)` mint a probe role of each violation shape inside a plpgsql subtransaction unwound by a `raise`, `(i4)` asserts neither leaked; QA reproduced Sec's 2026-08-08 failure demonstration at authoring rather than porting the green result. Six sabotage inversions confirm the intended assertion reds and the others do not — two are load-bearing: **allowlisting one of our own roles leaves `(i1)` GREEN and reds only `(i7)`** (the exact silent-defeat scenario the positive negatives exist for), and **neutering the fence leaves `(i1)` GREEN and reds only `(i2)`/`(i3)`**. **Two findings measured at authoring:** the `pg_` prefix is genuinely **reserved** (SQLSTATE **`42939`**), so the fence's `pg_%` exclusion is a filter and not a hiding place — asserted at `(i5)`; and **`pg_has_role` must not be "simplified" into a `pg_auth_members` join** — against a *bare* superuser (`rolsuper = t`, `rolbypassrls = f`) the mandated shape catches and the join is **blind**, and that role reads `nav_value`. That consequence is **not assertable in-battery** (`postgres` is not a superuser on this stack and cannot mint one), so `(i6)` pins only the premise and carries the out-of-band method runnable. **Sec joint-review AMBER → GREEN** — the security substance was GREEN throughout under independent mutation testing; the AMBER was entirely the Sec-owned artifact edit, including an antecedent collision that made `docs/SECURITY/index.html` read *"Sec still owes the non-vacuity review — QA owns it"*, review ownership reversed. **§10 three-axis cross-check CLEAN. Governance FLAT: §10 = 3 · Decision-3 = 16 labeled / 13 DDL-realized · DEFINER allowlist = 4 committed / 3 authored** — the three new functions are `pg_temp` test helpers carrying no DEFINER clause. `DESIGN.md` gains **§15**, three methodology entries whose common shape is that a green can be produced by an instrument, a specimen, or a mutant that cannot discriminate: *a differential whose **witness** satisfies both branches of a disjunction proves nothing about either*; *a **mutant** easier to catch than the edit you fear produces a green you cannot bank* (Sec's, self-reported against its own review instrument — it **deleted** a disjunct while the feared edit **substitutes** one); and the generalisation **an assertion sited next to a hazard is not thereby a fence against it** — measured, the membership-join swap passes the entire 74-assertion battery.

### v1.169 — 2026-08-08

**Phase 6 — SD-24's cross-tenant claim bounded to the application tier (PR #341, merge `7e0663f`).** `docs/SECURITY/index.html` asserted net-worth data is *"never cross-tenant"* with **no tier qualifier anywhere in the artifact**. Measured on the local PG 17 stack that claim is true of the application tier and false of the DB-admin tier, so SD-24 now carries a **TIER BOUNDARY** paragraph scoping it, and names `pfin.fn_nav_series` (`062`) as the §2.1.2 read surface with its EXECUTE posture. **Every credential this project provisions is clean** — `pfin_etl` / `authenticator` / `anon` cannot read `nav_value`; `service_role` is fenced at **both** layers (no `pg_read_all_data` membership, plus `054`'s column grant withholding `nav_value`). Three measured corrections to the obvious reading: **`rolbypassrls` is not the mechanism** (reach comes from `pg_read_all_data` membership or ownership; all 27 `pfin` tables are `relforcerowsecurity = f`, so revoking BYPASSRLS *alone* is inert while reading as a fix); **`rolpassword IS NULL` is not a fence** (`pg_hba` decides authenticability — a `host all all 127.0.0.1/32 trust` line let a passwordless role read cross-tenant); and **the first attempt to test the claim passed falsely**, because a probe from the host arrives as `192.168.65.1` and matches the `scram-sha-256` line rather than the `trust` line. The realistic hazard is human, not adversarial: `supabase_read_only_user` has a password, can log in, and reads every tenant's financial trajectory — behind a name that invites *"give the accountant read-only access."* **RT-31 leg (i)** is specified here and **owned by QA to author**: a **set-complement** fence over unexpected `rolbypassrls` / `pg_read_all_data` holders, never a per-role deny-list, demonstrated to fail closed against two probe roles in a rolled-back transaction. *0 rows shows the fence passes; it does not show the fence can fail* — reproduce the failure when authoring, do not port the green result.

### v1.168 — 2026-08-08

**Phase 6 — A1 scheduled unfiltered dependency audit + cron-liveness check (PR #340, merge `4c58944`).** F/CTO-ratified **A1**: `scheduled-dep-audit.yml` audits all three tracked manifests daily at **06:17 UTC** at `--audit-level=moderate`, accumulating across directories rather than failing fast. Two files, +283 lines, **no existing fence re-scoped** — A1's whole value is that it cannot regress anything. **Why a cron and not a filter change:** the failure mode is time-based — advisories publish and get patched in the registry with no commit in this repo, and both existing venues are commit-triggered (`scanner-npm-audit` unfiltered but high+critical only; `dependency-audit` at moderate but `api/**`-filtered), so neither fires during a quiet period, which is exactly when the exposure accrues. Off-the-hour scheduling is deliberate per GitHub's documented drop behaviour at high load. The job carries an **inversion probe** — a throwaway tree pinned to `nanoid@3.3.16` asserting the same command exits non-zero — because a fence whose expected state is green forever with nobody looking is the most likely of all to degrade silently; proven to fire (stubbing the audit → FATAL; weakening the threshold to `critical` → FATAL). Plus **`scheduled-audit-liveness`** in the unfiltered workflow, because A1 has a silent fail-open of its own: public repos auto-disable scheduled workflows after 60 days of inactivity (this repo is **public**, verified), and scheduled runs can be delayed or dropped while still enabled — **in both cases nothing goes red**. It queries the workflow's `state` directly rather than deducing an absence, is conclusion-agnostic (a *failing* scheduled run still proves the cron is alive), and lives outside the cron because **nothing inside a cron can report its own absence**. All six branches exercised, three via a `gh` shim.

### v1.167 — 2026-08-08

**Phase 6 — the `api` audit fence's stale baseline corrected; the npm range-union and path-filter traps recorded (PR #338, merge `e7b79eb`).** Comment-only, proven mechanically rather than by eyeballing the diff (stripping every `#` line from both revisions leaves no difference). **The lead finding reverses an earlier suspicion:** the worry that `@sveltejs/kit 2.70.2` was a "fix" landing *inside* the advisory's vulnerable range dissolved when measured against `gh api /advisories/GHSA-29g2-3rmr-qm68`, which gives `vulnerable_version_range: <= 2.70.1` and `first_patched_version: 2.70.2`. **npm collapses every advisory reaching a package into one node and prints the widest range across all of them stamped with the highest severity** — the rendered `<=2.70.2` was a composite of the moderate ReDoS and the low `cookie` advisory that reads exactly like one. Three corrections land: the fence's comment asserted *"the 3 pre-existing LOW-severity advisories"* — a tally that is a live property of the **registry advisory DB**, not of the lockfile, so it goes stale with no commit here; the block now **names the advisory and carries no tally**, with an instruction to measure rather than recall. The range-union trap is recorded with its worked example. And the **path-filter gap** is recorded: an `api/**`-filtered job reports the state of the last commit that touched that path, not of the tree, so a newly published advisory leaves `main` reading green until some PR happens to touch `api/` and inherits a failure it did not cause — which is exactly what happened to #337. Bounded honestly: nothing above `high` can hide this way, but **moderate-only findings can**.

### v1.166 — 2026-08-08

**Phase 6 — `scanner-npm-audit` enumerates from `git ls-files`; paired nested-prune inversion check (PR #339, merge `e3b2578`).** The `find . -name 'package.json' -not -path './node_modules/*'` guard matched **only the root** — `./api/node_modules/*` and `./workers/provider-sync/node_modules/*` were never excluded. **Why nobody noticed: on a fresh CI checkout there is no `node_modules` at all**, so the pattern behaved identically to a correct one — the job was green for the right answer by accident of its environment, the instrument unable to observe the property it stood for. On a warm worktree it is not close: **314 paths enumerated against an intended 3**, auditing every vendored dependency's own manifest, each resolvable against the registry and each able to pass or fail for reasons unrelated to this repo. Fixed by enumerating from `git ls-files`, which cannot see `node_modules` at any depth because it is gitignored — removing the whole class of nested-prune bugs rather than patching the instance. Ships with a **paired inversion check** that plants a `package.json` inside a nested `node_modules` and fails closed if it is enumerated; without it a future refactor back to `find` would restore the 314-vs-3 behaviour **and still exit 0 on every cold runner**, shipping the regression unseen exactly as this one did. Both enumerators run end-to-end against a warm worktree: new → 3 enumerated, probe ignored, exit 0; old restored → 314, probe picked up, FATAL.

### v1.162 — 2026-08-07

**Phase 6 — the TimeZone sweep query fenced against drift; R3's production gate raised as a decision request (PR #330, merge `7d13568`).** **Part A (built):** a fail-closed CI fence asserting the role-sweep query is identical across its two homes — `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §4.1 and `supabase/tests/01_session_timezone.sql` (T3) — needing no production reach, since it is a text comparison over two committed files. **The identity claim was already false when it was written**, and both missing clauses were load-bearing: without `s.setrole <> 0` the runbook's copy matched `061`'s own database-level pin and hit §4.1's **STOP** on the very declaration it exists to confirm; without the `unnest` + anchored `c ilike 'timezone=%'` it selected `s.setconfig` wholesale, and that row carries `app.settings.jwt_secret` — **on production it would have printed the live JWT signing secret into the operator's terminal, the Coolify deploy log, and any Discord notification body.** Both found by *running* the sweep, not by reading it. Measured across all four clause combinations: `setrole <> 0` is the only clause closing the false positive; **either** clause independently closes the disclosure; neither is redundant. **Prose is not a fence.** The fence enforces exactly what the artifacts claim and nothing stronger — it normalizes whitespace (the two copies are byte-different by construction), asserts no particular clause (that would red on something never promised, and false reds get fences deleted), anchors structurally rather than on content (anchoring on the query text would make it blind to the drift it exists to catch), and its one content check guards the **extraction**, not the query. Fail-once proven in **three** directions including whitespace-stays-green, because a fence proven only to RED has not been shown to red *for the right reason*. Verified by extracting each `run:` block and executing it verbatim — which is how a real bug in this PR was caught: the extraction-guard step originally pointed at a `README.md` that does not exist in this repo, and would have exited 2 rather than 1 on its first run. **Part B — the R3 production gate — is a decision request for the F/CTO, not a proposal to merge**; nothing here implements it.

### v1.158 — 2026-08-06

**Phase 6 — ADR-044 ACCEPTED: the as-of/TimeZone two-clock hazard, R1+R2 sequenced (PR #326, merge `d4e1152`).** The canonical record of the F/CTO's R1+R2 ruling (2026-08-04, on the Architect brief), landed once both halves of R1 had merged (#324, #325). Doc-only, one file. **Records a decision; does not schedule the R2 build** — the work-spec's §7-or-Linear home is left as an open F/CTO question rather than pre-empted. **The crux is that R1 and R2 answer different questions:** `061` guarantees **which** day "today" is, for every container and login role, from one declaration; `fn_server_today()` guarantees that **both sides of one comparison** use the same day, and says nothing about which day or about anything across containers. **The finding that decided it, in neither prior framing:** `pfin.nav_daily.nav_date` is `current_date` in the **ETL worker's** session — a different container and login role (`pfin_etl`) from the web app (`authenticator`) — on an **append-only** table whose entire ADR-040 justification is that the series is unrecomputable. **R2 cannot make the worker's "today" equal the app's; only the pin can.** And the stronger form found after the work-spec was written: **R2 does not relieve ADR-043 either** — it makes the gate self-consistent *in the session zone* while `closedAtLabel` renders hard-pinned UTC. **For ADR-043 the pin is the sole load-bearing dependency, under every path, permanently.** The correction owed to whoever re-points `060` when R2 lands is recorded **verbatim** because a paraphrase loses it: *"no longer the app's within-comparison guarantee,"* **never** *"no longer load-bearing."* Three queued items discharged, including the **§10 boundary — the pin does NOT enter the catalogued ledger**: Decision 4's membership predicate is *security-load-bearing*, and multi-layer is the **method**, not the membership test; the TZ pin is financial-correctness-load-bearing with no privilege boundary, credential, admission channel or isolation predicate, and `PGTZ` in a container env is a misconfiguration, not an adversary. Cataloguing it would widen the predicate to *"anything fenced at multiple layers"* — **a ledger that admits everything discriminates nothing.** Ruled with both required halves attached: `layer ≠ class` (RT-27's *subject* is a credential-admission channel; its *layer* is network/config), and the honest counter (the pin's half-pinned-inspects-clean property **is** the Decision 1 clause (d) detectability class — but **detectability-failure is a property the fence HAS, not the class of surface it PROTECTS**). Also records the compound sweep defect against `061` rather than as a sweep bug, with the transferable lesson stated: **the measurement that proves one thing safe is the one that proves another thing unsafe, and nobody joins them because they live in different artifacts with different authors.**

### v1.157 — 2026-08-06

**Phase 6 — ADR-043 amended, ratified a third time, and instrumented (PR #325, merge `ab5fea0`).** Second of a sequenced pair — `061` landed in #324; this is the correctness claim that depends on it. **The ordering was the point:** a document may forward-reference a document, but **a correctness claim must not forward-reference the mechanism that makes it true**; merging this first would have put a ratified financial-correctness claim on `main` whose named dependency was absent from the repo — the zone UTC *by image default, not by declaration* — precisely the condition **E1** exists to flag, live inside the ADR that adds E1. **The defect: a byte-exact quote of a false clause.** Reason 1 quoted `059` byte-exactly — *"V1 consumers pass CURRENT_DATE"* — and appended *"so both sides sit on one clock."* Measured: **zero app callers pass `CURRENT_DATE`**; `p_as_of` was `todayIso()` in the Node process. The quotation verifies, the citation resolves, the attribution is right, **and the conclusion is still false — every verbatim check passes.** And fixing the quotation alone would have produced a fourth false description, because the gloss did not follow *even if the quoted clause had been true*: **Lock 15 mod #2 guarantees the date is SERVER-derived and says nothing about WHICH SERVER**, and Node and Postgres are two servers with two clocks. Two independent defects, the second surviving the first being fixed — which is why the support is **struck, not reworded**, with the strike left visible. **Three ratifies against three descriptions, and only the third was true** (2026-08-04 on a false cost; 2026-08-04 on reason 1's false quote, found by Sec a day later; **2026-08-05** on the amended ADR): *a ratify against a false description is not a ratify*, and **two ratifies against two false descriptions do not sum to one good one** — which is why this was routed back as a third ratify rather than landed as an editorial amendment. Sec's blocking condition discharged: the database-timezone pin is named as the dependency (necessary-not-sufficient, with `PGTZ` at `source=client` and role overrides at `source=user` both outranking `source=database`), **E1** added so a justification falsifiable by a *deployment setting* no longer waits on a *user* action, and the `060` pairing made mutual (`060` 11× / `061` 6×, from zero). QA authored `api/src/lib/account-display.test.ts` — 10 assertions, superseding the file's own *"no test guards any of the above."* Full suite reconciled rather than reported: 60 files, 564 passed.

### v1.156 — 2026-08-06

**Phase 6 — the database TimeZone pinned to UTC by declaration (PR #324, merge `eb7e5da`; migrations `060` + `061`).** The app derives every NAV as-of date in the **Node** process as `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — unconditionally UTC — while Postgres evaluates `closed_at::date` in the **database session's** TimeZone. **Two clocks in two processes**, agreeing if and only if the session zone is UTC. Under a zone east of UTC a just-closed account stays in the §2.1.1 NAV headline and the open-account count until the zones re-converge; **west of UTC fails *safe*, which is worse**, because the defect becomes hemisphere-dependent and a US-based reviewer cannot reproduce it. Nothing errors in either direction. Before this branch the guarantee rested on a `060` clause **false in both halves** — the property was holding **locally by accident, which is why it survived review.** **The finding that justified the branch:** the local cluster was carrying `TimeZone=Asia/Tokyo` **on the `authenticator` role** — the LOGIN role PostgREST connects as, i.e. every web request — while `postgres` (what `pg_prove` and any manual inspection uses) read `UTC | database`. **The suite returned PASS throughout**: the *half-pinned deployment that INSPECTS CLEAN* shape `061`'s own header warns about, observed on a real stack rather than hypothesized. Role-level GUCs apply at login from the authenticating role and do not re-apply on `SET ROLE`, so `ALTER ROLE authenticated SET TimeZone` is a **no-op** while the same on `authenticator` moves every Data API request — **pinning or inspecting `authenticated` protects nothing.** `061` is stated as **necessary, not sufficient**: `PGTZ` resolves at `source = client` (and **`TZ` alone is a no-op**, the reassuring wrong instinct) and role-level overrides at `source = user`, both outranking the pin — so the full condition is the declaration **AND** no client `PGTZ` **AND** no role override, the latter two carried by the runbook TZ-1 gate and the `.env.example` prohibitions. Also closes a defect **this branch armed**: before `061` the `setrole = 0` row held only the two `app.settings.*` entries, so §4.1's `ilike '%timezone%'` sweep matched nothing — **`061` is what put a timezone entry into the row that carries the JWT signing secret**, and the sweep was genuinely safe on `main` and would have become unsafe the instant the pin landed. Caught by running it, in the only window where running it could catch it. Plus a **branded as-of type** moving the deferred user-supplied-as-of hazard from a sentence into a compile error.

### v1.155 — 2026-08-05

**Phase 6 — the `058` closed-account write fence classified at every app write path (PR #323, merge `e9dae70`).** Migration `058` attaches `fn_block_write_closed_account` to three tables and its raise carries the exact remedy — *reopen, correct, re-close* — which the app was **discarding** in favour of "please try again". **A closed account does not become writable by retrying; it becomes writable by being reopened** — the database was supplying the right answer and the app was throwing it away. The classifier is **exported and shared**, not copied per call site, and matches on the `write blocked:` prefix rather than the word "closed", since three fences in this schema use "closed" for three different subjects. **Fence coverage verified exhaustively rather than assumed:** all three app paths that INSERT into `account_trans` are wired; `writeSplitSet` / `upsertAnnotation` target tables `058` does not fence and `writeSplitSet`'s `account_trans` touch is a `.select()`; no app path writes the two checkpoint tables (grep-verified). The new battery asserts against `058`'s **verbatim** raise string and includes the negative case — a different raise still falls through to the generic envelope — **so the suite is not invariant to the thing it tests.** Also fixes three close-form defects and resolves #320's carried-over `GATE_LEG_MESSAGE['post-closure']` string, which read *"Close it as of a later date"* — **a live false instruction**, since the form posts `reason_code` only and no date control exists. **Scope changed mid-review:** this branch previously carried ADR-043 as well; Sec returned **AMBER on the ADR-043 half only** and it was split to its own branch, inverting the merge-order relationship an earlier revision of the body had claimed. What remained here was reviewed **GREEN**.

### v1.154 — 2026-08-05

**Phase 6 — a read failure must not claim "no accounts" (PR #320, merge `c3383aa`).** `const hasAccounts = !countErr && (count ?? 0) > 0` turned **any** read error into *"no accounts"*, so a user with a real net worth saw the new-user onboarding screen. §7.9 named this the worst instance but asked only for the `is_active` break, which #319 fixed — **the shape survived.** The prior behaviour was deliberate, not a slip (`netWorth.test.ts` carried an explicit fail-soft test), so the superseded contract is recorded in place with why fail-soft was originally chosen as well as why it is superseded. **The principle says *when* the distinction matters rather than asserting it always does: fail-soft is fine when the degraded value is inert; `hasAccounts: false` is not inert — it drives a screen that tells the user they own nothing.** Landed as `accountPresence: 'some' | 'none' | 'unknown'` rather than the `/accounts` precedent's boolean pair, because applied literally that precedent leaves `{#if !data.hasAccounts}` compiling and still rendering onboarding on a read error — **the fix would have depended on the same reviewer attention that missed this twice.** The precedent's shape is safe *because its payload is inert*; copying the silhouette inherits the look and not the safety, and the divergence is stated in-file so nobody harmonises it back. **The branch order was half the defect** — `+page.svelte` tested `!hasAccounts` **before** `netWorth === null`, the one position where the count could override evidence that outranks it; fixing the type without the order would have left a narrower, harder-to-find version of the same bug. Presence is load-bearing only at `netWorth === 0`, and is deliberately **not** derived server-side from a non-zero NAV: *a field that says "the read failed" is never wrong; a field that says "I deduced it from something else" is wrong the moment the something else moves.* Carries an unreachable-state detector (logged without the amount, per #318 F8), a badge obligation recorded on the type where the next person widening it will be standing, and a **second, unrelated change flagged up front** — PM and UX's copy ruling on the strings that shipped provisional in #319, two of which were required rather than taste (one named *"the closing date"*, a control that does not exist).

### v1.153 — 2026-08-05

**Phase 6 — BACKLOG §5.7's UTC deferral is one feature away, not indefinite (PR #322, merge `bbe9100`).** Two corrections to the per-user-timezone entry landed in #321; the second is about **risk class** and is the one that matters. The entry read *"blocked on the tenant-zone question"*, which reads as *safe to ignore* — but `059`'s own `closed_at` column comment says the dependency is **narrowing, not stable**: `059` **struck** the ADR-039 sound-only-at-`current_date` constraint and thereby **legalised past as-of dates**, so a §2.1.2 trajectory passing a user-chosen date is now a sanctioned path. **The trigger is the next feature that takes a user-chosen date**, and whoever picks the entry up **may be the trigger rather than someone waiting for it.** Second, the entry framed the item as date *rendering* — a wall-clock surprise — when the as-of half is an **inclusion difference on a net-worth read**. Both findings arrived by relay; they were verified against `059`'s comment **verbatim rather than acted on from the relay**, and the claim turned out **stronger** than the relay carried — the argument for checking rather than a reason to distrust the relay.

### v1.152 — 2026-08-05

**Phase 6 — PRD §2.4.1 / §2.4.2 re-pointed off the retired inactive-flag model (PR #321, merge `806c368`).** Discharges BACKLOG §7.7's PRD entry: `059` merged with #319, so the PRD was describing a column that no longer exists — the state that entry specifically warns is **worse than known-stale, because it is simply false rather than dated.** §2.4.2's mark-inactive sentence is replaced by the ratified model (closing is a **dated bookkeeping event, not a visibility flag**; permitted only when the account holds nothing as of the closing date and has no later-dated activity; history retained; no further entries accepted **including provider-originated ones**; reopening ungated; corrections via reopen → correct → re-close; the reason user-supplied and **never defaulted**). §2.4.1's inactive-flag clause is **rewritten, not deleted**, per the entry's own AC — ADR-042 **relocated** that capability to connect-time selection rather than withdrawing it, so deleting it would misreport a live capability as absent — and it adds that **closing is not un-sharing.** **The go-ahead was missing a blocker:** the entry's AC also required an Architect ruling on whether ADR-042 supersedes or preserves ADR-013 A1–A3 / PM-1, named only in the AC body, so **an entry with two blockers read as single-blocked from every summary of it**; reading the AC verbatim before drafting is what surfaced it, and it turned out already discharged inside ADR-042's *Preserved, not superseded* section. Plus a self-catch against the same AC — a first draft described the gate as measuring quantity held rather than market value, true and load-bearing in the UI copy but **the gate's check**, which the AC places in ARCH/SECURITY territory. Downstream, the Phase 2 UX flows still carry the retired pattern in four places including a **F/CTO-ratified Flag PM-1 resolution** that ADR-042 half-supersedes; booked to §7.7 with UX Designer as owner, because the AC requires *recording* the supersession rather than silently rewriting a ratified decision.

Four assertions were found passing while proving nothing, including one asserting a function absent **on a function `059` never creates** and one that was `is(f(d,true), f(d,true))`, unfailable. Coverage changes on Sec-reviewed blocks flagged rather than buried, including a Sec case with no assertion anywhere — reported, not silently fixed.
